## Assessment method and reconciliation

- **Covered well:** substantive explanations, examples, selection/trade-offs and failure treatment at the requested target depth. Specific local corrections can still be recorded separately.
- **Partial:** substantive material exists, but the row names a concrete missing depth or implementation/failure detail.
- **Mention only:** a superficial definition, incidental example, heading or tool name. External links do not supply lesson coverage.
- **Missing:** no substantive local treatment found after reading every source. A general-purpose capability does not automatically satisfy its AI-, framework- or language-specific form.
- **Incorrect/outdated:** accuracy findings are an overlay on coverage, not an extra mutually exclusive count. See corrections and batch observations.

Every row is now reconciled across the entire collection. In particular, Node chapters supply substantial testing, architecture, security, database and performance coverage absent from shorter frontend notes. Older contradicting claims are retained as local correction work, not misreported as complete topic absence. Equivalent frameworks/tools count fairly; Express satisfies the backend-framework requirement and one appropriate test/query tool is sufficient.

The 710 rows preserve the expanded user checklist for traceability, with routing, controllers and services graded separately from framework selection. Slash fragments inherit the area and preceding phrase: for example, “Server” under Next.js means Server Components, and “Direct” under AI security means direct prompt injection. They are not new unrelated requirements. Each row counts once for documentation inventory only; counts are not weighted by risk, effort or employability. AI-specific missing rows are separate from transferable backend foundations. Borderline breadth is conservatively Partial or Mention only. No Unassessed rows remain.

Depth keys used in each row: **S-FE** senior frontend implementation/debugging/trade-offs and complete accessible flows; **I-BE** intermediate backend reliable implementation and failure handling; **I-OPS** intermediate practical deployment/operation; **S-ENG** senior frontend ownership with intermediate backend architecture; **I-AI** intermediate applied-AI building/evaluating/operating; **AI-SE** strong constrained/independently verified assistant workflow; **I-PY** intermediate Python for AI Solution track; **Optional** role-dependent awareness.

## Prioritized gaps

### Critical

1. Correct reusable examples before copying them: concurrency stop policy, worker completion, JSONL null/size handling, Zod PATCH defaults, auth claim/parsing/error boundaries, shutdown and error-log behavior. Correct TypeScript-runtime-validation and React render/effect misconceptions.
2. Build senior frontend practice around one complete management interface: typed URL/server state, mutation recovery, complex forms/drafts, accessible dialog/table, localization and measured profiling. Repair memoization/identity examples rather than adding more optimization slogans.
3. Add modern Next.js App Router server/client and authorization/data/cache boundaries. Current generic SSR and Pages Router material does not cover these requirements.
4. Start applied AI with a bounded model-backed application, representative evaluations, recoverable failures, permission-aware retrieval and controlled tools. Add MCP and AI-assisted workflow evidence. Course links are resources, not coverage.
5. Prove intermediate backend capability with the existing curriculum: run real database invariants and negative auth tests, implement a webhook/job, deploy and restore. No senior distributed-systems implementation standard is imposed.

### Next

- Advanced TypeScript modelling and assertions, explicit React snapshot/batching/effect/race exercises, and complete data-query/form workflows.
- Semantic/native browser fundamentals and modern CSS cascade/layout/stacking/container patterns with actual debugging evidence.
- Component-library API/token/versioning and Stencil/custom-element lifecycle/attribute/property/event/accessibility integration.
- OpenAPI, SQL subqueries/CTEs/window functions, concrete cursor pagination, signed/deduplicated webhooks, scheduling and broker adapters.
- Reproducible Docker/cloud/IAM deployment, private source-map handling, backup/restore procedure and cost/alert configuration.
- Python foundations plus one tested async validated AI endpoint for the requested track.

### Optional / role-dependent

Deep Kubernetes administration, complex sagas/fencing, dedicated MLOps/fine-tuning infrastructure, graph-database specialization, Streamlit and multiple competing agent frameworks are not immediate prerequisites. Keep awareness, then deepen when a project/role requires it. Core LLM operation, evaluation, permission boundaries and cost budgets are immediate applied-AI priorities; Python is specifically requested but need not block a TypeScript-first prototype.

## Confirmed incorrect or outdated explanations



These corrections were checked against official project documentation or standards. Sources were accessed on the review date. Local quotations are restricted to the relevant claim; none of the original notes were changed.


### N1. ES-module microtask order is presented as universal

Note: [docs/node-js/module-01-nodejs-foundations.md:1250](</Users/Noemi_Verebelyi/Desktop/learning-notes/docs/node-js/module-01-nodejs-foundations.md:1250>).

The sample says nextTick runs before promise and queueMicrotask. That ordering is not universal at ES-module top level: ESM evaluation already runs in the microtask queue. State the CJS/ESM execution context and show both outputs. An isolated local ESM run printed promise, queueMicrotask, nextTick. This matters for event-loop reasoning and brittle order assertions.

Official reference: [documentation](https://nodejs.org/api/process.html#when-to-use-queuemicrotask-vs-processnexttick).


### N2. Concurrency limiter does not stop assigning after failure

Note: [docs/node-js/module-01-nodejs-foundations.md:1460](</Users/Noemi_Verebelyi/Desktop/learning-notes/docs/node-js/module-01-nodejs-foundations.md:1460>).

Promise.all rejects the aggregate; it does not stop the other worker loops. A local reproduction with limit=2 and mapper(0) rejecting still started items 1, 2, 3 and 4. Add shared stop/cancellation state before taking another item and define whether to await/drain running workers. This contradicts the explanation and can cause unwanted writes after the caller sees failure.

Official reference: [documentation](https://tc39.es/ecma262/multipage/control-abstraction-objects.html#sec-promise.all).


### N3. Module classification omits modern syntax detection

Note: [docs/node-js/module-01-nodejs-foundations.md:398](</Users/Noemi_Verebelyi/Desktop/learning-notes/docs/node-js/module-01-nodejs-foundations.md:398>).

The categorical table/default statements need a versioned caveat: recent Node.js can detect ESM syntax in ambiguous .js input without an explicit package type. Prefer explicit type and .cjs/.mjs for predictability. Do not teach absent type as an unconditional CJS rule.

Official reference: [documentation](https://nodejs.org/api/packages.html#syntax-detection).


### T1. TypeScript interfaces do not validate network data

Note: [docs/senior_growth/TypeScript/What_kind_of_problems_could_be_solved_by_using_TS?:63](</Users/Noemi_Verebelyi/Desktop/learning-notes/docs/senior_growth/TypeScript/What_kind_of_problems_could_be_solved_by_using_TS?:63>).

Static declarations are erased and do not check incoming JSON. Parse unknown input with a runtime schema or a real validator, then derive or assign a domain type. An assertion or annotated response is not validation. Otherwise malformed API data passes into trusted code.

Official reference: [documentation](https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/2/basic-types.html#erased-types).


### T2. PropTypes guidance is not valid for current React

Note: [docs/senior_growth/TypeScript/Prop_Types_or_typescript.md:6](</Users/Noemi_Verebelyi/Desktop/learning-notes/docs/senior_growth/TypeScript/Prop_Types_or_typescript.md:6>).

prop-types is a separate package, not a built-in import from React. React 19 removed React propTypes checks and silently ignores them. Explain legacy development-time warnings separately from runtime boundary validation. TypeScript also cannot replace runtime checks at trust boundaries.

Official reference: [documentation](https://react.dev/blog/2024/04/25/react-19-upgrade-guide#removed-proptypes-and-defaultprops).


### T3. ES3 is listed as a current TypeScript target

Note: [docs/senior_growth/TypeScript/Can_we_write_code_in_TS_if_we_would_like_to_support_older_browsers.md:12](</Users/Noemi_Verebelyi/Desktop/learning-notes/docs/senior_growth/TypeScript/Can_we_write_code_in_TS_if_we_would_like_to_support_older_browsers.md:12>).

TypeScript 5.5 removed target ES3. Keep the useful syntax-versus-polyfill distinction, but specify compiler versions, module output, dependency browser baselines and actual legacy-browser verification. An ES5 target alone does not guarantee the entire modern app runs in an old browser.

Official reference: [documentation](https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-5-5.html#disabling-features-deprecated-in-typescript-50).


### A1. WCAG 2.2 is incorrectly labelled a draft

Note: [docs/senior_growth/A11y/What_is_WCAG_standard__What_parts_it_contains_Web_Content_Accessibility_Guidelines.md:39](</Users/Noemi_Verebelyi/Desktop/learning-notes/docs/senior_growth/A11y/What_is_WCAG_standard__What_parts_it_contains_Web_Content_Accessibility_Guidelines.md:39>).

WCAG 2.2 is a W3C Recommendation. Use the applicable conformance target and test specific success criteria, including focus not obscured, dragging alternatives, target size and accessible authentication. The inspected W3C publication is dated December 2024. Do not imply that generic practices or an audit score alone establish conformance.

Official reference: [documentation](https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/).


### A2. Focus trapping is overgeneralized

Note: [docs/senior_growth/A11y/Can_we_test,_measure_a11y_somehow.md:44](</Users/Noemi_Verebelyi/Desktop/learning-notes/docs/senior_growth/A11y/Can_we_test,_measure_a11y_somehow.md:44>).

Contain keyboard focus for a modal dialog; do not trap ordinary dynamic content or nonmodal UI. Implement initial focus, a visible close mechanism, Escape handling, background inertness and return focus. Distinguish native controls from composite widget arrow-key models.

Official reference: [documentation](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/dialog-modal/).


### A3. The u element is incorrectly described as removed

Note: [docs/senior_growth/A11y/Which_tags_did_HTML_5_introduce.md:58](</Users/Noemi_Verebelyi/Desktop/learning-notes/docs/senior_growth/A11y/Which_tags_did_HTML_5_introduce.md:58>).

The current HTML Standard defines u for an unarticulated non-textual annotation. It is not a generic styling tool, but it is not removed. Separate obsolete markup from semantic elements and historical additions.

Official reference: [documentation](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/text-level-semantics.html#the-u-element).


### C1. CSS capability comparison is outdated

Note: [docs/senior_growth/CSS/CSS_vs_SASS_vs_Styled_Components_vs_CSS_Modules.md:24](</Users/Noemi_Verebelyi/Desktop/learning-notes/docs/senior_growth/CSS/CSS_vs_SASS_vs_Styled_Components_vs_CSS_Modules.md:24>).

CSS supports custom properties, native nesting, and built-in functions such as calc/min/max/clamp. Sass still provides compilation-time programming and mixins; compare those capabilities rather than claiming native CSS has no variables or nesting. The table conflicts with the repository’s own custom-property examples.

Official reference: [documentation](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-variables-1/).


### C2. CSS units conflate CSS and device pixels

Note: [docs/senior_growth/CSS/Difference_between_pixels,_ems_or_rems_Which_one_we_should_use.md:11](</Users/Noemi_Verebelyi/Desktop/learning-notes/docs/senior_growth/CSS/Difference_between_pixels,_ems_or_rems_Which_one_we_should_use.md:11>).

A CSS px is a reference unit, not necessarily one hardware pixel. em generally follows the element’s computed font size; em in font-size is resolved relative to the parent. Browser zoom can scale pixel-sized content. Test zoom/reflow instead of treating px as categorically unscalable.

Official reference: [documentation](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#font-relative-lengths).


### C3. New Sass examples use deprecated imports

Note: [docs/senior_growth/CSS/What_are_your_suggestions_to_someone_who_is_hesitating_to_use_SASS_What_are_the_key_feature_that_should_use.md:17](</Users/Noemi_Verebelyi/Desktop/learning-notes/docs/senior_growth/CSS/What_are_your_suggestions_to_someone_who_is_hesitating_to_use_SASS_What_are_the_key_feature_that_should_use.md:17>).

Sass @import was deprecated in Dart Sass 1.80. Teach @use and @forward with namespaces for new code; keep @import only as legacy migration context.

Official reference: [documentation](https://sass-lang.com/documentation/breaking-changes/import/).


### R1. State and Effects are confused with render impurity

Note: [docs/senior_growth/React/Are_all_functional_components_pure.md:89](</Users/Noemi_Verebelyi/Desktop/learning-notes/docs/senior_growth/React/Are_all_functional_components_pure.md:89>).

Render must remain idempotent and free from nonlocal side effects. Having state or scheduling an Effect does not itself make render impure. State, props and context are render inputs. Memoization is an optimization, not a repair for impure rendering.

Official reference: [documentation](https://react.dev/reference/rules/components-and-hooks-must-be-pure).


### R2. New object creation is described as scheduling a React update

Note: [docs/senior_growth/React/Change_Detection_in_React.md:29](</Users/Noemi_Verebelyi/Desktop/learning-notes/docs/senior_growth/React/Change_Detection_in_React.md:29>).

Creating const newState = {...oldState} does not schedule a render. Calling the setter does. Separate render from commit and browser paint; a parent render can invoke a child even if the child’s props are unchanged. Do not teach an always-minimal tree edit or single DOM operation as a general guarantee.

Official reference: [documentation](https://react.dev/learn/render-and-commit).


### R3. A portal is not CSS or accessibility isolation

Note: [docs/senior_growth/React/Modals_in_react,_portal_or_not.md:15](</Users/Noemi_Verebelyi/Desktop/learning-notes/docs/senior_growth/React/Modals_in_react,_portal_or_not.md:15>).

A portal changes DOM placement; it does not provide a Shadow DOM boundary, remove global CSS, or implement dialog semantics and focus behavior. The shown modal lacks role/name, Escape handling, focus containment, background inertness and focus restoration. Native dialog or a correctly configured accessible library is a valid alternative.

Official reference: [documentation](https://react.dev/reference/react-dom/createPortal).


### R4. CRA deprecation is softened to an unofficial practical status

Note: [docs/senior_growth/React/build_tools.md:90](</Users/Noemi_Verebelyi/Desktop/learning-notes/docs/senior_growth/React/build_tools.md:90>).

Create React App was officially deprecated for new apps on 14 February 2025. Describe it as legacy/maintenance context and base new-app choices on requirements. Do not prescribe it as the current quick-prototype default.

Official reference: [documentation](https://react.dev/blog/2025/02/14/sunsetting-create-react-app).

Deprecated-but-still-supported APIs are distinguished from removed APIs.

### Reconciliation of provisional audit observations

- **N10 withdrawn as a categorical bug:** numeric strings such as `"3000"` are normalized as ports by Node's implementation. `app.listen(process.env.PORT ?? 3000)` is not inherently a Unix-socket bug. Explicit number/range validation remains better configuration practice; nonnumeric strings can select a path. A socket-listen experiment was blocked with `EPERM`; inspection of the installed Node24.18 `net` normalization confirmed the distinction. No server test is claimed to have passed.
- **QUERY is accurate:** [RFC10008](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc10008.html) was published June2026 and defines safe/idempotent content-bearing queries. This was checked rather than dismissed as unfamiliar syntax. Adoption caveat in the note is appropriate.
- **MikroORM7 claims are partly accurate:** moved legacy/ES decorator entry points and balanced loading are confirmed by [the migration guide](https://mikro-orm.io/docs/upgrading-v6-to-v7). The actionable issue is incomplete setup/version coherence, not those facts.
- **Path traversal is covered:** module09 explicitly describes symlinks and stronger server-generated names. Module02's short lexical example still needs that cross-reference; the entire corpus is not missing path security.
- **HTTP/security/database contradictions are reconciled positively:** later Node chapters correctly explain PATCH idempotency, HTTP reuse, CORS, OAuth/OIDC, HttpOnly limits, CAP/ACID and safe migrations. Earlier errors remain local corrections; they do not erase accurate coverage elsewhere.
- **Examples versus placeholders:** pseudocode such as `classifyError`, `validateRecords` or an injected repository is treated as an interface-shaped illustration when stated as such. A missing helper is not automatically a defect; a claimed complete example with internally wrong behavior is.
- The appendix records all batch observations, including lower-priority precision/editing opportunities. It is an audit trail, not an extra list of proven vulnerabilities. No new package was installed, and examples requiring databases/provider accounts were statically reviewed, not claimed executed.

### Isolated verification performed

Minimal reproductions used local Node **v24.18.0**, outside the project, without installing dependencies or editing learning examples:

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| ESM scheduling | promise → queueMicrotask → nextTick |
| Limiter after one mapper rejection, limit2 | All inputs0–4 still started through the surviving worker |
| Worker exits0 without sending message | Wrapper remained pending; bounded diagnostic race reported it |
| JSONL null then object | ERR_STREAM_PUSH_AFTER_EOF; null treated as stream EOF |
| Regex /t(e)(st)/g over test1test2 | Matches test, test; digits not in match |
| Sorted comma-joined filenames | Different lists [a,b; c] and [a; b,c] serialize identically |
| Lowercase bearer scheme | Shown regex rejected it |
| application/jsonjunk | Shown prefix check accepted it |
| WeakMap with a nonregistered Symbol key | Accepted |

These are focused falsification checks, not execution of every lesson or completed project tests. The unmodified repository has documentation scripts; an application build, real database integration, browser E2E or provider experiment was not performed for this read-only source audit. No false pass is claimed for the blocked socket bind.

## Practice tasks and acceptance evidence



| Task | Proves | Concrete acceptance evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Accessible React management screen | Senior FE, state/forms/accessibility | Typed filters in the URL, server sort/pagination, selection across pages, an editable multistep draft, schema + server validation, unsaved-change navigation, a real accessible confirmation dialog, localization. Include keyboard/VoiceOver observations and automated checks; test 200%/400% zoom, reflow and reduced motion. |
| React race and identity lab | Senior React reasoning | A slow earlier search response cannot overwrite a later result; unmount cancels/ignores pending work; keys preserve edited row identity; show functional-update batching and a stale closure bug. Use controlled deferred responses and explain every fix. |
| Type-safe API boundary | TS senior depth | Treat network JSON as unknown, validate at runtime, model loading/success/failure as discriminated unions, require exhaustive handling, and prove malformed input rejection. Include negative compile-time cases outside existing restricted tests. |
| Intermediate Node service | Intermediate backend/full-stack | Use Express or equivalent with PostgreSQL, real-database integration tests, ownership/tenant authorization and negative tests, transactional invariants, one safe upload or signed webhook, bounded external calls, structured request logs, readiness and graceful shutdown. Reuse course exercises rather than learning another framework first. |
| Concurrency and streaming failure lab | Runtime competence | Reject one worker and assert the documented stop/drain policy; split UTF-8 across chunks; bound frame size; fail the output midstream; cancel; verify bounded memory and that the process terminates. |
| Deploy and recover the app | Intermediate operations | Container with nonroot runtime and externally supplied secrets; isolated database, migration plan, TLS endpoint, health/readiness, logs/metrics, budget, backup plus a demonstrated restore; exercise rollback after a failed release. |
| Permission-aware document assistant | Intermediate applied AI | Ingest/update/delete documents; compare lexical and hybrid retrieval; filter access before context assembly; cite stable document spans; abstain with insufficient evidence. Stream with cancellation, validate outputs, cap time/cost and show recoverable provider failures. |
| AI evaluation and agent tool lab | AI reliability/security | A small curated versioned dataset covering normal, missing-evidence, cross-tenant, injection and tool-failure cases; deterministic checks + a human rubric, repeated runs and a baseline. Limit tool arguments/permissions, make writes idempotent, recover after interruption, record redacted traces and per-task cost. |
| AI-assisted issue implementation record | Strong AI-assisted engineering | Start from an issue specification and acceptance criteria; document context and secret exclusions; use a local branch/worktree where useful; review the actual diff for invented APIs and scope creep; map requirements to checks and retain failures/corrections. No remote push: only the user may push. |
| Python companion service | Intermediate Python, role dependent | Reimplement one small validated ingestion/model endpoint with FastAPI/Pydantic or equivalent, pinned environment, async timeouts, structured logs, pytest + integration tests, document parsing limits and deployment notes. |

These are proposed exercises. No completion, quality level or capability is inferred from their presence in this report or in the original notes. For actual Autocode coursework, use the supplied build/test scripts and Module 4 Cobertura command, preserve src/tests, and close all handles.

## Detailed batch audit trail

The following observations were persisted after each batch. They retain exact reading boundaries and smaller findings. The final matrix and correction/qualification sections above supersede preliminary grades, ambiguous IDs and N10's withdrawn categorical port claim. “Next” language in old batch notes describes the historical reading sequence, not outstanding work.

## Continued audit — B01

Re-read all 10 CSS files, 1,458 numbered lines, without truncation. Reconfirmed C1–C3 and recorded C4–C6. Styling organization/tool trade-offs are substantive; layout/cascade/modern responsive mechanisms remain shallow in this folder. Final absence judgments wait for cross-folder reconciliation.

- `docs/senior_growth/CSS/CSS_vs_SASS_vs_Styled_Components_vs_CSS_Modules.md`, through line 195: C1 reconfirmed at 24,113–115: native CSS capabilities misrepresented. Scope/build/runtime comparisons substantive but partial; add cascade behavior, dynamic custom properties and SSR trade-offs.
- `docs/senior_growth/CSS/Can_you_describe_what_is_CSS_Specificity.md`, through line 102: B01-C4: 9–43 gives basic selector ranking; 33–34 overgeneralizes precedence without origin/importance/layer/scope context. Missing :is/:not/:has/:where, inheritance, layers and a real cascade-debugging exercise.
- `docs/senior_growth/CSS/Difference_between_pixels,_ems_or_rems_Which_one_we_should_use.md`, through line 110: C2 reconfirmed at 11,29,77–79: CSS px versus hardware pixels; em resolution depends on property. No clamp/min/max/dynamic viewport examples.
- `docs/senior_growth/CSS/Do_you_still_think_BEM_style_is_valid_nowadays_Main_benefits.md`, through line 95: BEM explanation and examples 5–9,53–78 substantive; no enforcement/migration/collision case. Naming is not hard isolation.
- `docs/senior_growth/CSS/Do_you_use_any_CSS_methodology_or_have_any_guidelines_for_it_on_your_project.md`, through line 93: BEM, modules, token organization and stylelint discussed 11–78. Statements about our project are narrative, not a checked implementation or demonstrated practice.
- `docs/senior_growth/CSS/How_can_you_fit_your_website_for_different_screen_sizes_(css_media_queries,_breakpoints).md`, through line 123: Media-query examples 14–81 and custom properties 99–109 substantive; container queries only mentioned at111; no container-type/@container example. Responsive-image sizing is not srcset/sizes/picture coverage.
- `docs/senior_growth/CSS/How_would_you_organize_CSS_on_huge_project.md`, through line 305: Component/global structure and dark token switch substantive at7–135. B01-C5:31 suggests breakpoint custom properties without explaining they cannot substitute into media query conditions. No token versioning or component API contracts.
- `docs/senior_growth/CSS/What_are_your_suggestions_to_someone_who_is_hesitating_to_use_SASS_What_are_the_key_feature_that_should_use.md`, through line 132: C3 reconfirmed: deprecated Sass @import17–19. Mixins/extends/operators demonstrated; no @use/@forward or selector-expansion trade-off.
- `docs/senior_growth/CSS/index.md`, through line 14: Navigation only; earns no topic coverage.
- `docs/senior_growth/CSS/styling_react_apps.md`, through line 289: B01-C6: 223–289 presents one hybrid stack as universal modern best practice without requirements/evidence; treat as an option. Card TSX265–275 has implicit-any destructured props under strict mode. SSR/dynamic-theme compatibility mentioned, not worked through.
